# Runtime availability

Generated by `catalog/tools/query.py --build-report` from each runtime's `releases.json`, `gaps.json`, `limitations.json` and `download-log.jsonl`.

## python on linux: available

| Runtime | Major | Versions | Architectures | Downloaded here | OS support at the time | Limitations |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| python | 3.8 | 3.8.20 | amd64, arm64 | 3.8.20 | source tarball only from python.org; python-build-standalone (third-party) adds prebuilt amd64/arm64 archives starting this major | still no free-threading or true parallel threads for Python code -- the GIL is unchanged; no structural pattern matching (3.10), no exception groups (3.11); last major with reliable stock support on Windows 7/8/8.1 |
| python | 3.9 | 3.9.23 | amd64, arm64, armv7, ppc64le, riscv64, s390x | 3.9.23 | source tarball only from python.org; python-build-standalone adds amd64/arm64/armv7/ppc64le/riscv64/s390x archives | 3.9.0 itself has no Apple Silicon/arm64 macOS build -- 3.9.1 or later is required for that; Windows ARM64 at this point is an embeddable zip, not a full installer (that's 3.11); no structural pattern matching (3.10); dropped Windows Vista and 7 outright |
| python | 3.10 | 3.10.21 | amd64, arm64, armv7, ppc64le, riscv64, s390x | 3.10.21 | source tarball only from python.org; python-build-standalone covers amd64/arm64/armv7/ppc64le/riscv64/s390x | match/case is new syntax -- code using it cannot run on 3.9 or earlier at all, not just a library-availability problem; still no exception groups (3.11); still GIL-bound; free-threading isn't even experimental yet |
| python | 3.11 | 3.11.16 | amd64, arm64, armv7, ppc64le, riscv64, s390x | 3.11.16 | source tarball only from python.org; python-build-standalone covers amd64/arm64/armv7/ppc64le/riscv64/s390x | tomllib is read-only -- no TOML writer in the standard library; no free-threading or JIT yet (3.13); no template strings (3.14) |
| python | 3.12 | 3.12.14 | amd64, arm64, armv7, ppc64le, riscv64, s390x | 3.12.14 | source tarball only from python.org; python-build-standalone covers amd64/arm64/armv7/ppc64le/riscv64/s390x | distutils removal breaks old packaging code that still imports it directly -- must migrate to setuptools; PEP 695 generics syntax is new and unusable on 3.11 and earlier; still no free-threaded build (that's 3.13, experimental) |
| python | 3.13 | 3.13.15 | amd64, arm64, armv7, ppc64le, riscv64, s390x | 3.13.15 | source tarball only from python.org; python-build-standalone covers amd64/arm64/armv7/ppc64le/riscv64/s390x | free-threading is experimental, has a substantial single-threaded performance hit, and breaks any C extension not rebuilt for it; the JIT is experimental, off by default, and its own docs describe the performance gains so far as modest; several stdlib modules developers relied on for decades (cgi, telnetlib, ...) are simply gone, not deprecated-but-present |
| python | 3.14 | 3.14.7 | amd64, arm64, armv7, ppc64le, riscv64, s390x | 3.14.7 | source tarball only from python.org; python-build-standalone covers amd64/arm64/armv7/ppc64le/riscv64/s390x | free-threading is officially supported but still optional/non-default -- most C extensions still need an explicit free-threaded rebuild; t-strings are new syntax: a Template object isn't a str, so tools and libraries built around f-strings don't understand it for free; the JIT introduced in 3.13 is still experimental in 3.14, not promoted alongside free-threading |

Milestones: async_await since 3.5; native_threads since 2.0; true_parallelism since 2.6; tls_1_2 since 3.4; tls_1_3 since 3.7; ipv6 since 2.2; unicode_default since 3.0; package_manager_bundled since 3.4; generics since 3.9; 64bit_windows since 2.5; arm64_macos since 3.9; arm64_windows since 3.9; arm64_linux since 3.8

16 recorded gaps, e.g.: 2.0 any: no prebuilt/portable Linux binary: python.org ships no Linux binaries for any Python version, and python-build-standalone only builds CPython 3.8 and later; only the source tarball (kind=source) is available for this major; 2.1 any: no prebuilt/portable Linux binary: python.org ships no Linux binaries for any Python version, and python-build-standalone only builds CPython 3.8 and later; only the source tarball (kind=source) is available for this major; 2.2 any: no prebuilt/portable Linux binary: python.org ships no Linux binaries for any Python version, and python-build-standalone only builds CPython 3.8 and later; only the source tarball (kind=source) is available for this major

## python on macos: available

| Runtime | Major | Versions | Architectures | Downloaded here | OS support at the time | Limitations |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| python | 2.4 | 2.4.3 | universal | 2.4.3 | PowerPC/Intel universal installer ("macpython" build) | no with-statement or conditional (ternary) expression until 2.5; no absolute/relative import control until 2.5 (PEP 328); packaging still distutils + separately-installed easy_install; no pip |
| python | 2.5 | 2.5.0 – 2.5.4 (5) | universal | 2.5.4 | universal (PowerPC/Intel) installer | with-statement needs a future-import in 2.5 itself (default from 2.6); no importlib; the import system is still C-level only; packaging still distutils/easy_install, no pip |
| python | 2.6 | 2.6.0 – 2.6.6 (4) | universal | 2.6.6 | universal installer, now stating a Mac OS X 10.3 minimum | GIL still serializes threads; multiprocessing is the only way to use multiple cores; no TLS SNI support in ssl (arrives 2.7.9); no async I/O framework (asyncio is 3.4) |
| python | 2.7 | 2.7.0 – 2.7.18 (19) | amd64, universal | 2.7.17, 2.7.18 | installers spanning Mac OS X 10.3, 10.5, 10.6 and (from 2.7.9-era builds) 10.9 minimums over its lifetime; no build ever supported Apple Silicon | ssl had no SNI (Server Name Indication) or modern certificate-verification defaults until 2.7.9 (2014-12, PEP 466) -- a real problem for HTTPS clients on name-based virtual hosting before then; no async/await; only generator-based coroutine patterns (send/throw from 2.5); str is 8-bit bytes and unicode is a separate type -- the classic Python 2 encode/decode footguns; official end-of-life 2020-01-01; code still targeting 2.7 today runs on an unsupported, unpatched interpreter |
| python | 3.1 | 3.1.0 – 3.1.4 (4) | universal | 3.1.4 | universal installer, Mac OS X 10.3 minimum | third-party package availability under Python 3 is still very thin; no importlib-based import system yet; no stdlib venv (virtualenv is still third-party only) |
| python | 3.2 | 3.2.0 – 3.2.5 (6) | universal | 3.2.5 | universal installers, Mac OS X 10.3 and (separately) 10.6 minimum variants | no yield from (3.3); no stdlib venv -- virtualenv is still third-party (3.3); no asyncio; only low-level select/selectors-based I/O; the GIL rewrite improved fairness between threads but did not add multi-core parallelism for Python code |
| python | 3.3 | 3.3.0 – 3.3.5 (6) | universal | 3.3.5 | universal installers, Mac OS X 10.5 and 10.6 minimum variants | no asyncio yet (provisional in 3.4); no pip bundled by default (ensurepip is 3.4); no async/await syntax (3.5) |
| python | 3.4 | 3.4.0 – 3.4.4 (5) | universal | 3.4.4 | universal installers, Mac OS X 10.5 and 10.6 minimum variants | asyncio's API is explicitly provisional and changed across 3.4.x point releases -- not a stable target yet; no async/await syntax -- asyncio coroutines are written with `@asyncio.coroutine` and `yield from`; pathlib is also provisional at this point |
| python | 3.5 | 3.5.0 – 3.5.4 (5) | universal | 3.5.4 | Mac OS X 10.6 and later | asyncio and typing are both still provisional APIs and can change without a deprecation cycle; no f-strings (3.6); dict ordering is not guaranteed by the language yet (official in 3.7); dropped Windows XP support in the stock installer -- XP is only reachable via unofficial/backport builds |
| python | 3.6 | 3.6.0 – 3.6.8 (9) | amd64, universal | 3.6.8 | Mac OS X 10.9 (amd64 installer) alongside a 10.6-minimum universal installer | dict ordering is only a CPython implementation detail here, not a language guarantee -- other implementations weren't required to match it; typing module is still evolving rapidly; no dataclasses (3.7), no breakpoint() (3.7) |
| python | 3.7 | 3.7.0 – 3.7.9 (10) | amd64, universal | 3.7.6, 3.7.9 | Mac OS X 10.9 (amd64 installer) alongside a 10.6-minimum universal installer | TLS 1.3 support in ssl is preliminary/experimental and depends on OpenSSL 1.1.1, which wasn't finalized when 3.7.0 shipped; making async/await reserved keywords breaks any code still using them as variable names; no walrus operator or positional-only parameters (3.8) |
| python | 3.8 | 3.8.0 – 3.8.20 (12) | amd64, arm64, universal | 3.8.20 | Mac OS X 10.9 installer, plus python-build-standalone amd64/arm64 archives | still no free-threading or true parallel threads for Python code -- the GIL is unchanged; no structural pattern matching (3.10), no exception groups (3.11); last major with reliable stock support on Windows 7/8/8.1 |
| python | 3.9 | 3.9.0 – 3.9.23 (15) | amd64, arm64, universal | 3.9.23 | 10.9+ generally; full Apple Silicon (arm64) support and the universal2 fat-binary installer arrived specifically in the 3.9.1 patch release, not 3.9.0 | 3.9.0 itself has no Apple Silicon/arm64 macOS build -- 3.9.1 or later is required for that; Windows ARM64 at this point is an embeddable zip, not a full installer (that's 3.11); no structural pattern matching (3.10); dropped Windows Vista and 7 outright |
| python | 3.10 | 3.10.0 – 3.10.21 (13) | amd64, arm64, universal | 3.10.21 | macOS 11 (Big Sur) baseline for the universal2 installer; python-build-standalone covers amd64/arm64 archives | match/case is new syntax -- code using it cannot run on 3.9 or earlier at all, not just a library-availability problem; still no exception groups (3.11); still GIL-bound; free-threading isn't even experimental yet |
| python | 3.11 | 3.11.0 – 3.11.16 (11) | amd64, arm64, universal | 3.11.16 | macOS 11 (Big Sur) baseline for the universal2 installer; python-build-standalone covers amd64/arm64 | tomllib is read-only -- no TOML writer in the standard library; no free-threading or JIT yet (3.13); no template strings (3.14) |
| python | 3.12 | 3.12.0 – 3.12.14 (12) | amd64, arm64, universal | 3.12.14 | macOS 11 (Big Sur) baseline for the universal2 installer; python-build-standalone covers amd64/arm64 | distutils removal breaks old packaging code that still imports it directly -- must migrate to setuptools; PEP 695 generics syntax is new and unusable on 3.11 and earlier; still no free-threaded build (that's 3.13, experimental) |
| python | 3.13 | 3.13.0 – 3.13.15 (16) | amd64, arm64, universal | 3.13.15 | macOS 11 (Big Sur) baseline for the universal2 installer; python-build-standalone covers amd64/arm64 | free-threading is experimental, has a substantial single-threaded performance hit, and breaks any C extension not rebuilt for it; the JIT is experimental, off by default, and its own docs describe the performance gains so far as modest; several stdlib modules developers relied on for decades (cgi, telnetlib, ...) are simply gone, not deprecated-but-present |
| python | 3.14 | 3.14.0 – 3.14.7 (8) | amd64, arm64, universal | 3.14.7 | macOS 11 (Big Sur) baseline for the universal2 installer; python-build-standalone covers amd64/arm64 | free-threading is officially supported but still optional/non-default -- most C extensions still need an explicit free-threaded rebuild; t-strings are new syntax: a Template object isn't a str, so tools and libraries built around f-strings don't understand it for free; the JIT introduced in 3.13 is still experimental in 3.14, not promoted alongside free-threading |

## node on aix: available

| Runtime | Major | Versions | Architectures | Downloaded here | OS support at the time | Limitations |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| node | 6 | 6.7.0 – 6.17.1 (35) | ppc64 | — |  | no async/await (needs 7.6); no ES modules; no worker_threads; Windows XP and Vista officially dropped as of this release -- 4.x/5.x had still supported them |
| node | 7 | 7.0.0 – 7.10.1 (17) | ppc64 | — |  | async/await needs a runtime flag until 7.6.0; no ES modules; no worker_threads; non-LTS, 8-month lifespan |
| node | 8 | 8.0.0 – 8.17.0 (36) | ppc64 | — |  | ES modules require the --experimental-modules flag and .mjs extension; N-API is experimental -- no ABI stability guarantee yet; http2 is experimental and warns; no worker_threads |
| node | 9 | 9.0.0 – 9.11.2 (18) | ppc64 | — |  | ES modules still flagged/experimental; N-API still experimental; no worker_threads; OpenSSL 1.0.2, no TLS 1.3 |
| node | 10 | 10.0.0 – 10.24.1 (42) | ppc64 | — |  | worker_threads still needs --experimental-worker and is experimental; ES modules still need --experimental-modules; no TLS 1.3 (OpenSSL 1.1.0 doesn't have it -- needs 1.1.1); 32-bit Linux (x86) downgraded from Tier 1 to Experimental this release |
| node | 11 | 11.0.0 – 11.15.0 (17) | ppc64 | — |  | worker_threads not yet stable; ES modules still flagged; no TLS 1.3; non-LTS, 8-month lifespan |
| node | 12 | 12.0.0 – 12.22.12 (51) | ppc64 | — |  | ES modules still print ExperimentalWarning; N-API version 4 only; arm64 Windows is Experimental only; no global fetch (needs 18) |
| node | 13 | 13.0.0 – 13.14.0 (17) | ppc64 | — |  | Windows 7 / Server 2012 (non-R2) demoted to Experimental -- no longer an officially supported target; ES modules still experimental with warnings; worker_threads: no structured-clone support for every JS type yet |
| node | 14 | 14.0.0 – 14.21.3 (46) | ppc64 | — |  | dynamic import() / ESM loader hooks still experimental; top-level await not yet available outside ESM tricks; Windows 8.1/2012 R2 remains the floor -- unchanged from 13; no worker_threads structured-clone parity with the main thread for all types |
| node | 15 | 15.0.0 – 15.14.0 (18) | ppc64 | — |  | still no fetch or global Web Streams; no Apple Silicon support at all -- Mac binaries are x64-only and must run under Rosetta on M1 hardware; non-LTS, 8-month lifespan |
| node | 16 | 16.0.0 – 16.20.2 (37) | ppc64 | — |  | Windows 8.1/Server 2012 R2 demoted to Experimental -- Windows 10+ is now the supported floor; Apple Silicon builds are Experimental at the initial 16.0.0 release; no global fetch yet; corepack itself is experimental and opt-in via `corepack enable` |
| node | 17 | 17.0.0 – 17.9.1 (15) | ppc64 | — |  | OpenSSL 3.0 breaks some legacy crypto usage (certain algorithms/PBKDF configs) unless --openssl-legacy-provider is passed; no global fetch yet; non-LTS, 6-month lifespan |
| node | 18 | 18.0.0 – 18.20.8 (38) | ppc64 | — |  | fetch is experimental and prints a warning; no HTTP proxy support in fetch; OpenSSL 3 legacy-provider friction persists for some crypto users; still needs `corepack enable` for pnpm/yarn; Intel Mac floor (10.15) had not risen to match Apple Silicon's 11 yet |
| node | 19 | 19.0.0 – 19.9.0 (13) | ppc64 | — |  | fetch still experimental (stable only from 21); no require(esm) yet; non-LTS, 8-month lifespan |
| node | 20 | 20.0.0 – 20.20.2 (41) | ppc64 | — |  | fetch remains experimental until 21; permission model experimental and incomplete (not all subsystems honor it); macOS 10.15 (Catalina) support dropped -- 11+ required from here on |
| node | 21 | 21.0.0 – 21.7.3 (13) | ppc64 | — |  | non-LTS, 8-month lifespan; no require(esm) yet -- CommonJS still cannot require() a synchronous ESM-only package |
| node | 22 | 22.0.0 – 22.23.2 (35) | ppc64 | — |  | require(esm) still needed a flag at GA (unflagged later, in 22.12.0); TypeScript support is type-stripping only, never type-checking, and only through experimental flags at this point; 32-bit Windows support was still nominally present but was dropped two majors later, at 24 |
| node | 23 | 23.0.0 – 23.11.1 (14) | ppc64 | — |  | still no type-checking -- type-stripping only; enums, namespaces, and experimental decorators still need the separate --experimental-transform-types flag; non-LTS, 8-month lifespan |
| node | 24 | 24.0.0 – 24.21.0 (29) | ppc64 | — |  | 32-bit Windows (x86) is no longer an officially supported platform at all; 32-bit ARM Linux (armv7) downgraded to Experimental; macOS 11-13.4 no longer supported -- 13.5 is the new floor, a much bigger jump than prior majors; type stripping still can't type-check or handle enums/namespaces without --experimental-transform-types |
| node | 25 | 25.0.0 – 25.9.0 (14) | ppc64 | — |  | corepack is no longer distributed with Node.js by default -- `npm install -g corepack` (or a package manager's own installer) is now required; this ends the bundling era that ran from 14.19/16.9 through 24.x and will break CI/Docker images that assumed `corepack enable` just worked; non-LTS, 8-month lifespan |
| node | 26 | 26.0.0 – 26.9.0 (14) | ppc64 | — |  | http.Server.prototype.writeHeader() and the legacy internal _stream_* modules (_stream_wrap, _stream_readable, _stream_writable, _stream_duplex, _stream_transform, _stream_passthrough) are removed entirely -- old shims/polyfills touching them break; module.register() is now runtime-deprecated ahead of eventual removal; older POWER8 and pre-z14 mainframe hardware can no longer run official builds -- the AIX/ppc64/s390x floors were raised; as of this research date (2026-09-17) Node 26 is still Current-only -- it does not enter LTS until 2026-10-28, so treating it as production-stable ahead of that date is premature |

## go on aix: available

| Runtime | Major | Versions | Architectures | Downloaded here | OS support at the time | Limitations |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| go | 1.21 | 1.21.0 – 1.21.13 (14) | ppc64 | — |  | for-loop variables are created once and reused by every iteration -- capturing the loop variable in a closure or goroutine without an explicit per-iteration copy is a classic data race/bug (fixed in 1.22); cannot range over a function -- custom iterators must be hand-written as callback-taking functions rather than used with for-range (added 1.23) |
| go | 1.22 | 1.22.0 – 1.22.12 (13) | ppc64 | — |  | cannot range over a function -- custom iterators must be hand-written as callback-taking functions rather than used with for-range (added 1.23) |
| go | 1.23 | 1.23.0 – 1.23.12 (13) | ppc64 | — |  | generic type aliases only work within a single package -- cross-package generic aliases are not yet supported |
| go | 1.24 | 1.24.0 – 1.24.13 (14) | ppc64 | — |  |  |
| go | 1.25 | 1.25.0 – 1.25.14 (15) | ppc64 | — |  |  |
| go | 1.26 | 1.26.0 – 1.26.8 (9) | ppc64 | — |  | big-endian 64-bit PowerPC on Linux (linux/ppc64) is on its last release using the ELFv1 ABI -- Go 1.27 switches it to ELFv2 |
| go | 1.27 | 1.27.0 – 1.27.1 (2) | ppc64 | — |  |  |

## go on freebsd: available

| Runtime | Major | Versions | Architectures | Downloaded here | OS support at the time | Limitations |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| go | 1.2 | 1.2.2 | amd64, x86 | — | unchanged | no built-in HTTP/2 in net/http (added 1.6); no context package in the standard library -- cancellation/deadlines/request-scoped values need golang.org/x/net/context or hand-rolled channels (added 1.7); no module system at all -- dependencies must live under GOPATH/src with no version pinning (experimental modules added 1.11); no TLS 1.3 support of any kind in crypto/tls (opt-in support added 1.12) |
| go | 1.3 | 1.3 – 1.3.3 (4) | amd64, x86 | — | requires FreeBSD 8 or later; as of 1.3 the kernel must have COMPAT_FREEBSD32 configured; ARM requires FreeBSD 10 (switch to EABI syscalls) | no built-in HTTP/2 in net/http (added 1.6); no context package in the standard library -- cancellation/deadlines/request-scoped values need golang.org/x/net/context or hand-rolled channels (added 1.7); no module system at all -- dependencies must live under GOPATH/src with no version pinning (experimental modules added 1.11); no TLS 1.3 support of any kind in crypto/tls (opt-in support added 1.12) |
| go | 1.4 | 1.4 – 1.4.3 (3) | amd64, x86 | — | unchanged | no built-in HTTP/2 in net/http (added 1.6); no context package in the standard library -- cancellation/deadlines/request-scoped values need golang.org/x/net/context or hand-rolled channels (added 1.7); no module system at all -- dependencies must live under GOPATH/src with no version pinning (experimental modules added 1.11); no TLS 1.3 support of any kind in crypto/tls (opt-in support added 1.12) |
| go | 1.5 | 1.5 – 1.5.4 (5) | amd64 | — | requires FreeBSD 8-STABLE or later (new use of the SYSCALL instruction) | no built-in HTTP/2 in net/http (added 1.6); no context package in the standard library -- cancellation/deadlines/request-scoped values need golang.org/x/net/context or hand-rolled channels (added 1.7); no module system at all -- dependencies must live under GOPATH/src with no version pinning (experimental modules added 1.11); no TLS 1.3 support of any kind in crypto/tls (opt-in support added 1.12) |
| go | 1.6 | 1.6 – 1.6.4 (5) | amd64, x86 | — | now defaults to clang instead of gcc as the external C compiler | no context package in the standard library -- cancellation/deadlines/request-scoped values need golang.org/x/net/context or hand-rolled channels (added 1.7); no module system at all -- dependencies must live under GOPATH/src with no version pinning (experimental modules added 1.11); no TLS 1.3 support of any kind in crypto/tls (opt-in support added 1.12); TLS 1.3 exists but is opt-in only (GODEBUG=tls13=1) -- off unless a client/server explicitly enables it (default-on from 1.13) |
| go | 1.7 | 1.7 – 1.7.6 (6) | amd64, x86 | — |  | no module system at all -- dependencies must live under GOPATH/src with no version pinning (experimental modules added 1.11); no TLS 1.3 support of any kind in crypto/tls (opt-in support added 1.12); TLS 1.3 exists but is opt-in only (GODEBUG=tls13=1) -- off unless a client/server explicitly enables it (default-on from 1.13); no error wrapping -- fmt.Errorf has no %w verb and there is no errors.Is/As/Unwrap, so building/inspecting error chains means a third-party lib (e.g. pkg/errors) or manual type assertions (added 1.13) |
| go | 1.8 | 1.8 – 1.8.7 (8) | amd64, x86 | — |  | no module system at all -- dependencies must live under GOPATH/src with no version pinning (experimental modules added 1.11); no TLS 1.3 support of any kind in crypto/tls (opt-in support added 1.12); TLS 1.3 exists but is opt-in only (GODEBUG=tls13=1) -- off unless a client/server explicitly enables it (default-on from 1.13); no error wrapping -- fmt.Errorf has no %w verb and there is no errors.Is/As/Unwrap, so building/inspecting error chains means a third-party lib (e.g. pkg/errors) or manual type assertions (added 1.13) |
| go | 1.9 | 1.9 – 1.9.7 (8) | amd64, x86 | — |  | no module system at all -- dependencies must live under GOPATH/src with no version pinning (experimental modules added 1.11); no TLS 1.3 support of any kind in crypto/tls (opt-in support added 1.12); TLS 1.3 exists but is opt-in only (GODEBUG=tls13=1) -- off unless a client/server explicitly enables it (default-on from 1.13); no error wrapping -- fmt.Errorf has no %w verb and there is no errors.Is/As/Unwrap, so building/inspecting error chains means a third-party lib (e.g. pkg/errors) or manual type assertions (added 1.13) |
| go | 1.10 | 1.10 – 1.10.8 (9) | amd64, x86 | — | requires FreeBSD 10.3 or later (9.3 support removed) | no module system at all -- dependencies must live under GOPATH/src with no version pinning (experimental modules added 1.11); no TLS 1.3 support of any kind in crypto/tls (opt-in support added 1.12); TLS 1.3 exists but is opt-in only (GODEBUG=tls13=1) -- off unless a client/server explicitly enables it (default-on from 1.13); no error wrapping -- fmt.Errorf has no %w verb and there is no errors.Is/As/Unwrap, so building/inspecting error chains means a third-party lib (e.g. pkg/errors) or manual type assertions (added 1.13) |
| go | 1.11 | 1.11 – 1.11.13 (14) | amd64, x86 | — | c-shared/c-archive build modes now supported on freebsd/amd64 | no TLS 1.3 support of any kind in crypto/tls (opt-in support added 1.12); TLS 1.3 exists but is opt-in only (GODEBUG=tls13=1) -- off unless a client/server explicitly enables it (default-on from 1.13); no error wrapping -- fmt.Errorf has no %w verb and there is no errors.Is/As/Unwrap, so building/inspecting error chains means a third-party lib (e.g. pkg/errors) or manual type assertions (added 1.13); modules are opt-in (GO111MODULE=auto): a go.mod-less checkout under GOPATH still builds in legacy GOPATH mode (module-aware mode became the default, GOPATH mode retired, in 1.16) |

| go | 1.7 | 1.7 – 1.7.6 (6) | amd64 | 1.7.6 | adds support for macOS 10.12 Sierra (binaries built with pre-1.7 Go do not work correctly on Sierra) | no module system at all -- dependencies must live under GOPATH/src with no version pinning (experimental modules added 1.11); no TLS 1.3 support of any kind in crypto/tls (opt-in support added 1.12); TLS 1.3 exists but is opt-in only (GODEBUG=tls13=1) -- off unless a client/server explicitly enables it (default-on from 1.13); no error wrapping -- fmt.Errorf has no %w verb and there is no errors.Is/As/Unwrap, so building/inspecting error chains means a third-party lib (e.g. pkg/errors) or manual type assertions (added 1.13) |

| go | 1.16 | 1.16 – 1.16.15 (16) | amd64, arm64 | 1.16.15 | adds Apple Silicon support: darwin/arm64 (GOOS=darwin, GOARCH=arm64), full cgo/pie/race support; darwin/arm64 (iOS) renamed to ios/arm64, plus a new ios/amd64 simulator port; this is the last release that runs on macOS 10.12 Sierra -- Go 1.17 requires macOS 10.13 High Sierra+ | no generics -- type-parameterized containers/algorithms require interface{} plus type assertions/reflection, or per-type code generation (added 1.18); for-loop variables are created once and reused by every iteration -- capturing the loop variable in a closure or goroutine without an explicit per-iteration copy is a classic data race/bug (fixed in 1.22); cannot range over a function -- custom iterators must be hand-written as callback-taking functions rather than used with for-range (added 1.23); GO386=387 (x87-only float mode) removed; use GO386=softfloat on pre-SSE2 hardware |

| go | 1.13 | 1.13 – 1.13.15 (16) | amd64, x86 | 1.13.15 | unchanged | modules are opt-in (GO111MODULE=auto): a go.mod-less checkout under GOPATH still builds in legacy GOPATH mode (module-aware mode became the default, GOPATH mode retired, in 1.16); no //go:embed -- bundling static assets into the binary needs a third-party generator (e.g. go-bindata) or reading files from disk at runtime (added 1.16); no generics -- type-parameterized containers/algorithms require interface{} plus type assertions/reflection, or per-type code generation (added 1.18); for-loop variables are created once and reused by every iteration -- capturing the loop variable in a closure or goroutine without an explicit per-iteration copy is a classic data race/bug (fixed in 1.22) |

## php on linux: available

| Runtime | Major | Versions | Architectures | Downloaded here | OS support at the time | Limitations |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| php | 8.0 | 8.0.30 | amd64, arm64 | 8.0.30 | no official php.net binary; unofficial static-php-cli builds exist going back to this line | JIT gives little to no benefit for typical I/O-bound web/CMS workloads (it mainly helps CPU-bound/numeric code) and shipped disabled by default; no enums, readonly properties, or Fibers (8.1) -- so no first-class coroutine primitive yet; ReactPHP/Amp/Swoole still rely on generators or Swoole's own coroutine runtime (a separate C extension) rather than a language-level primitive; no intersection types or 'never' return type (8.1); this is the first major with any official Linux/macOS binaries in this catalog, and even those are the unofficial static-php-cli project, not php.net |
| php | 8.1 | 8.1.23 – 8.1.34 (11) | amd64, arm64 | 8.1.34 | no official php.net binary; static-php-cli unofficial builds available | Fibers are cooperative, not preemptive -- a fiber that never yields still blocks the whole process; there is still no OS-thread-based true parallelism without ext-parallel/pthreads on a ZTS build, or a separate process model (Swoole, php-fpm workers); no readonly promoted-constructor-property shorthand combined with all visibility combos until later patch/minor fixes; no enum interfaces' constants (8.2 refines), no readonly classes as a whole (8.2 is per-property only until 8.2's class-level readonly); JIT still opt-in and still mostly irrelevant to typical web workloads |
| php | 8.2 | 8.2.10 – 8.2.32 (21) | amd64, arm64 | 8.2.32 | no official php.net binary; static-php-cli unofficial builds available | no first-class support for 'never' unions beyond what 8.1 gave; no clone-with syntax (that is 8.5); JIT and Fibers unchanged from 8.1 -- still opt-in JIT, still cooperative-only concurrency |
| php | 8.3 | 8.3.0 – 8.3.32 (27) | amd64, arm64 | 8.3.32 | no official php.net binary; static-php-cli unofficial builds available | Windows 7/8 support was NOT dropped at this line -- that happens at 8.4 with the VS17 (Visual Studio 2022) toolset move; treat any claim that 8.3 drops Windows 7/8 as incorrect; no clone-with (8.5), no closures-in-const-expressions (8.5) |
| php | 8.4 | 8.4.1 – 8.4.23 (19) | amd64, arm64 | 8.4.23 | no official php.net binary; static-php-cli unofficial builds available | this is the line where Windows 7 and Server 2008 R2 stop being supported -- a machine that could run 8.3 may not be able to run 8.4; property hooks add call overhead versus plain public properties -- not a free abstraction; JIT still opt-in; still limited benefit for typical web workloads |
| php | 8.5 | 8.5.2 – 8.5.8 (7) | amd64, arm64 | 8.5.8 | no official php.net binary; static-php-cli unofficial builds available | still under a year old at research time (2026-09-17, released 2025-11-20) -- ecosystem/extension support (Swoole, Xdebug, etc.) for a fresh major typically lags by months; still no native async/await keyword or preemptive threading; Fibers plus userland event loops remain the concurrency model; JIT remains opt-in |

## php on windows: available

| Runtime | Major | Versions | Architectures | Downloaded here | OS support at the time | Limitations |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| php | 4.4 | 4.4.0 – 4.4.9 (10) | x86 | 4.4.9 | Windows 98/NT4/2000/XP/2003 era; x86 only, VC6-built binaries, no x64 build ever existed for this branch | objects behave like values (copied on assignment) rather than references, making OOP code fragile (fixed by Zend Engine 2 object handles in PHP 5.0); no exceptions before 5.0 (try/catch does not exist); no namespaces (5.3), no closures (5.3); no SPL, no PDO |
| php | 5.0 | 5.0.0 – 5.0.5 (6) | x86 | 5.0.5 | Windows 98/NT4/2000/XP/2003; x86 only, VC6-built, no x64 build | no namespaces (5.3) -- large codebases used prefix-based faux-namespacing (Zend_Db, PEAR_...); no closures/anonymous functions (5.3); callbacks used string/array callables; no late static binding (5.3), so 'self::' inside inherited static methods could not be overridden politely; no built-in unit testing, no Composer (2012) -- dependencies were vendored by hand or via PEAR |
| php | 5.1 | 5.1.0 – 5.1.6 (7) | x86 | 5.1.6 | Windows 98/NT4/2000/XP/2003; x86 only, VC6-built, no x64 build | no namespaces, closures, or late static binding (all land in 5.3); no Composer-era autoloading standard; spl_autoload_register appears in 5.1.2 but PSR-4 conventions came years later; no bundled opcode cache |
| php | 5.2 | 5.2.17 | x86 | 5.2.17 | Windows 2000/XP/2003/Vista; x86 only, VC6-built, no x64 build | no namespaces, closures, or late static binding (5.3); no Composer (2012); PEAR was the standard package channel; no bundled opcode cache; APC was the common third-party choice; last major before the long-lived 5.3 line; widely used but security-EOL since 2011 |
| php | 5.3 | 5.3.29 | x86 | 5.3.29 | Windows XP SP3/2003 and later; x86 only (no x64 build), VC9-built (Visual Studio 2008) | no generators/yield (5.5); no bundled opcode cache -- still relied on APC or commercial Zend Optimizer+; no scalar type hints or return types (7.0); closures could not be bound to arbitrary objects until Closure::bind/bindTo landed in 5.4 |
| php | 5.4 | 5.4.45 | x86 | 5.4.45 | Windows XP SP3/2003 and later; x86 only (no x64 build for this branch), VC9-built | no generators (5.5); no bundled opcode cache (5.5); no 64-bit Windows build at all -- large in-process arrays/strings were constrained to a 32-bit address space on Windows; no scalar/return type declarations (7.0) |
| php | 5.5 | 5.5.38 | amd64, x86 | 5.5.38 | Windows Vista/Server 2008 and later (Windows XP/Server 2003 support dropped -- first line requiring VC11/Visual Studio 2012 runtime); first line with an official x64 build, alongside x86 | OPcache ships but is opt-in (opcache.enable=0 by default) -- enabled-by-default arrives with 7.0; the new x64 Windows build still used a 32-bit internal integer type, so PHP_INT_MAX on Windows x64 was the same as on x86 until PHP 7's engine rewrite; no scalar/return type declarations (7.0); no Fibers/coroutine primitives -- async libraries of the era (ReactPHP, launched 2012) relied purely on callback-based event loops, not generators-as-coroutines patterns, until this release made generator-based coroutines practical |
| php | 5.6 | 5.6.40 | amd64, x86 | 5.6.40 | Windows Vista/Server 2008 and later; x86 and x64, VC11-built | OPcache still not enabled by default (7.0); TLS negotiation regressed: default STREAM_CRYPTO_METHOD_TLS_CLIENT was widened to include TLS 1.1/1.2 in 5.6.0-5.6.6, then a compatibility fix in 5.6.7 reverted the default to TLS 1.0-only until PHP 7.2 restored a modern default -- code relying on 'default means TLS 1.2' on 5.6.7+ needed to pass explicit crypto-method flags; no scalar/return type declarations, no null coalescing operator (both 7.0); last PHP 5 release; the 5.x object/array-copy-on-write engine is markedly slower than the phpng engine that shipped in 7.0 |
| php | 7.0 | 7.0.0 – 7.0.33 (34) | amd64, x86 | 7.0.33 | Windows 7/Server 2008 R2 and later; x86 and x64, VC14-built (Visual Studio 2015); true 64-bit integers on Windows x64 for the first time | no strict_types enforcement unless the file opts in with declare(strict_types=1) -- coercive mode (silent int/string juggling) is still the default everywhere; no nullable types or void return type (7.1); no object return type (7.2), no trailing commas in function calls (7.3); no arrow functions, typed properties, or JIT (all land 7.4/8.0) |
| php | 7.1 | 7.1.0 – 7.1.33 (34) | amd64, x86 | 7.1.33 | Windows 7/Server 2008 R2 and later; x86 and x64, VC14-built | no object return type (7.2); no arrow functions or typed properties (7.4); no JIT (8.0); TLS default still regressed to TLS-1.0-only for STREAM_CRYPTO_METHOD_TLS_CLIENT (fixed in 7.2) |
| php | 7.2 | 7.2.0 – 7.2.34 (35) | amd64, x86 | 7.2.34 | Windows 7/Server 2008 R2 and later (Windows Vista/Server 2008 support dropped with the move to VC15/Visual Studio 2017); x86 and x64 | no arrow functions or typed properties (7.4); no JIT (8.0); no Fibers (8.1); TLS 1.3 not available -- depends on OpenSSL 1.1.1, which ships with 7.4-era builds |
| php | 7.3 | 7.3.0 – 7.3.33 (34) | amd64, x86 | 7.3.33 | Windows 7/Server 2008 R2 and later; x86 and x64, VC15-built | no arrow functions, typed properties, or bundled OpenSSL 1.1.1/TLS 1.3 (all 7.4); no JIT (8.0), no Fibers (8.1) |
| php | 7.4 | 7.4.0 – 7.4.33 (32) | amd64, x86 | 7.4.33 | Windows 7/Server 2008 R2 and later; x86 and x64, VC15-built | no JIT (8.0); no union types, named arguments, or match expression (8.0); no enums, readonly properties, or Fibers (8.1); last PHP 7 release -- still using the pre-JIT Zend VM; CPU-bound workloads see no JIT speedup on this line |
| php | 8.0 | 8.0.0 – 8.0.30 (30) | amd64, x86 | 8.0.30 | Windows 7/Server 2008 R2 and later (Windows XP-era wording no longer applies; effectively 7+); x86 and x64, VS16-built (Visual Studio 2019) | JIT gives little to no benefit for typical I/O-bound web/CMS workloads (it mainly helps CPU-bound/numeric code) and shipped disabled by default; no enums, readonly properties, or Fibers (8.1) -- so no first-class coroutine primitive yet; ReactPHP/Amp/Swoole still rely on generators or Swoole's own coroutine runtime (a separate C extension) rather than a language-level primitive; no intersection types or 'never' return type (8.1); this is the first major with any official Linux/macOS binaries in this catalog, and even those are the unofficial static-php-cli project, not php.net |
| php | 8.1 | 8.1.0 – 8.1.34 (35) | amd64, x86 | 8.1.34 | Windows 7/Server 2008 R2 and later (Microsoft's own VS2019 toolset still targets back to 7 SP1); x86 and x64, VS16-built | Fibers are cooperative, not preemptive -- a fiber that never yields still blocks the whole process; there is still no OS-thread-based true parallelism without ext-parallel/pthreads on a ZTS build, or a separate process model (Swoole, php-fpm workers); no readonly promoted-constructor-property shorthand combined with all visibility combos until later patch/minor fixes; no enum interfaces' constants (8.2 refines), no readonly classes as a whole (8.2 is per-property only until 8.2's class-level readonly); JIT still opt-in and still mostly irrelevant to typical web workloads |
| php | 8.2 | 8.2.0 – 8.2.33 (34) | amd64, x86 | 8.2.33 | Windows 7/Server 2008 R2 and later per Microsoft's VS2019/VS16 toolset floor; x86 and x64, VS16-built | no first-class support for 'never' unions beyond what 8.1 gave; no clone-with syntax (that is 8.5); JIT and Fibers unchanged from 8.1 -- still opt-in JIT, still cooperative-only concurrency |
| php | 8.3 | 8.3.0 – 8.3.33 (33) | amd64, x86 | 8.3.33 | Windows 7/Server 2008 R2 and later per the VS16 (Visual Studio 2019) toolset this line still uses; x86 and x64 | Windows 7/8 support was NOT dropped at this line -- that happens at 8.4 with the VS17 (Visual Studio 2022) toolset move; treat any claim that 8.3 drops Windows 7/8 as incorrect; no clone-with (8.5), no closures-in-const-expressions (8.5) |
| php | 8.4 | 8.4.0 – 8.4.25 (26) | amd64, x86 | 8.4.25 | minimum bumped to Windows 8/Server 2012 (Windows 7/Server 2008 R2 support dropped with the move to VS17/Visual Studio 2022, whose v143 toolset no longer targets Windows 7); x86 and x64 | this is the line where Windows 7 and Server 2008 R2 stop being supported -- a machine that could run 8.3 may not be able to run 8.4; property hooks add call overhead versus plain public properties -- not a free abstraction; JIT still opt-in; still limited benefit for typical web workloads |
| php | 8.5 | 8.5.0 – 8.5.10 (11) | amd64, x86 | 8.5.10 | Windows 8/Server 2012 and later per the VS17 (Visual Studio 2022) toolset; x86 and x64 | still under a year old at research time (2026-09-17, released 2025-11-20) -- ecosystem/extension support (Swoole, Xdebug, etc.) for a fresh major typically lags by months; still no native async/await keyword or preemptive threading; Fibers plus userland event loops remain the concurrency model; JIT remains opt-in |

Milestones: modules since 5.3; generics since none; async_await since 8.1; native_threads since none; true_parallelism since none; tls_1_2 since 5.6; tls_1_3 since 7.4; http2_client since 5.5; http3_client since none; package_manager_bundled since none; 64bit_windows since 5.5; arm64_windows since none; arm64_macos since none; arm64_linux since none; unicode_default since none

6 recorded gaps, e.g.: 4.4 amd64: windows.php.net never shipped an x64 build for this branch (64-bit Windows PHP builds only began with the PHP 5.5 line); 5.0 amd64: windows.php.net never shipped an x64 build for this branch (64-bit Windows PHP builds only began with the PHP 5.5 line); 5.1 amd64: windows.php.net never shipped an x64 build for this branch (64-bit Windows PHP builds only began with the PHP 5.5 line)

Milestones: package_manager_bundled since 1.0; modules since 1.7; true_parallelism since 2.14; 64bit_windows since 2.11; long_vectors since 3.0; tls_1_2 since 3.2; byte_compiler_jit_default since 3.4; strings_as_factors_default_false since 4.0; raw_strings since 4.0; native_pipe since 4.1; lambda_syntax since 4.1; arm64_macos since 4.1; unicode_default since 4.2; arm64_linux since 3.0

1 recorded gaps, e.g.: 1.9 amd64: pre-2.0.1 macOS builds live under a different CRAN-archive layout (root-level version dirs like '1.9', '2.0', plus el-capitan/, mavericks/, universal/, powerpc/, i686/ subtrees) that this scrape did not walk

## ruby on linux: available

| Runtime | Major | Versions | Architectures | Downloaded here | OS support at the time | Limitations |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| ruby | 2.1 | 2.1.9 | amd64 | — | Source build or distro packages | Still no true parallel threads (GVL-bound); No pattern matching; No frozen_string_literal magic comment; Keyword arguments still implicitly convertible from/to a trailing Hash -- the breaking separation is 3.0 |
| ruby | 2.2 | 2.2.10 | amd64 | — | Source build or distro packages | Fixnum vs Bignum is still an implementation detail programs can observe (e.g. via #class) -- unified in 2.4; No JIT of any kind (MJIT arrives 2.6); TLS support depends entirely on whatever OpenSSL the Ruby build links against; no guarantee of TLS 1.3 (needs OpenSSL 1.1.1, which postdates this release) |
| ruby | 2.3 | 2.3.0 – 2.3.8 (9) | amd64, arm64 | — | Source build or distro packages | frozen_string_literal is opt-in per file; nothing is frozen by default (a later plan to default it to true was never shipped, including in 3.4/4.0); No JIT, no pattern matching, no Ractor; Original RubyInstaller's older MinGW toolchain is less current than RubyInstaller2's MSYS2 base used from 2.4 on |
| ruby | 2.4 | 2.4.0 – 2.4.10 (10) | amd64, arm64 | — | Source build or distro packages | Ractor, Fiber scheduler, YJIT and pattern matching all absent; RubyInstaller2's newer MSYS2-based toolchain targets a newer Windows baseline than the installer it replaced -- exact minimum Windows version for this specific major is inferred, not confirmed against an archived RubyInstaller2 changelog |
| ruby | 2.5 | 2.5.0 – 2.5.9 (10) | amd64, arm64 | — | Source build or distro packages | No JIT yet (2.6); No YJIT (3.1); TLS 1.3 needs the underlying OpenSSL to be 1.1.1+; Ruby's openssl gem doesn't vendor OpenSSL, so TLS 1.3 availability depends on how/when the interpreter was built, not the Ruby version alone |
| ruby | 2.6 | 2.6.0 – 2.6.10 (11) | amd64, arm64 | — | Source build or distro packages | MJIT requires a C compiler to be present on the machine at *runtime*, not just at build time -- a real constraint on minimal container images and on Windows; MJIT's compile/warmup overhead often outweighs its benefit outside numeric/game-style workloads; not a general web-app win; No YJIT (3.1); no Ractor (3.0); pattern matching still absent (2.7 experimental) |
| ruby | 2.7 | 2.7.0 – 2.7.8 (9) | amd64, arm64 | 2.7.8 | Source build or distro packages | Pattern matching is experimental and warns on use; its syntax still changed before stabilizing in 3.0; Keyword-argument/Hash auto-conversion still works but is deprecated -- code relying on it breaks outright in 3.0; No Ractor, no true parallelism; No Fiber scheduler / non-blocking IO integration |
| ruby | 3.0 | 3.0.0 – 3.0.7 (8) | amd64, arm64 | — | Source build or distro packages | Ractor is explicitly experimental: objects passed between Ractors must be shareable/frozen, most existing C extensions and gems are not Ractor-safe, and the main Ractor still has its own GVL; RBS/TypeProf are opt-in and unenforced -- there is no compiler-checked static typing, and most gems ship no RBS signatures; Keyword-argument separation breaks any code/gem still relying on implicit Hash-to-kwargs conversion from 2.7 and earlier -- one of the largest breaking changes in Ruby's history; Fiber Scheduler only helps code paths that go through it; C extensions doing blocking syscalls directly bypass it |
| ruby | 3.1 | 3.1.0 – 3.1.7 (8) | amd64, arm64 | — | Source build or distro packages; YJIT usable on x86-64 | YJIT at launch only supports Unix-like x86-64 -- no Windows, no arm64/Apple Silicon, no other CPU architectures; YJIT is off by default and still experimental; needs an explicit flag and extra memory for compiled code; Ractor still experimental with the same shareability constraints as 3.0; No default-on JIT of any kind for typical deployments |
| ruby | 3.2 | 3.2.0 – 3.2.11 (12) | amd64, arm64 | — | Source build or distro packages; plus an experimental WASI/WebAssembly build (ruby.wasm) for running Ruby in browsers/WASI runtimes | YJIT is production-ready but still requires an explicit --yjit flag; not on by default; The WASM/WASI build has no real OS threads, no TCP sockets, and a cut-down stdlib -- most gems with C extensions can't run there; Ractor still experimental; its API kept changing (overhauled again in 4.0); Apple Silicon (arm64 macOS) support remains community-provided (Homebrew, rbenv/ruby-build); ruby-lang.org still ships no official macOS binaries at all |
| ruby | 3.3 | 3.3.0 – 3.3.12 (13) | amd64, arm64 | — | Source build or distro packages; WASI/WebAssembly build continues | Prism is bundled but not yet the default parser -- you must pass --parser=prism to use it (default parser switch is 3.4); RJIT is explicitly documented as experimental/research-only, x86-64 Unix only, and slower than YJIT -- not a production JIT choice; M:N thread scheduler is opt-in and disabled by default on the main Ractor for C-extension compatibility; Ractor API still not finalized (replaced again in 4.0's Ractor::Port) |
| ruby | 3.4 | 3.4.0 – 3.4.10 (11) | amd64, arm64 | 3.4.10 | Source build or distro packages; WASI/WebAssembly build continues | The long-floated plan to make string literals frozen by default was NOT shipped in 3.4 (nor in 4.0) after ecosystem pushback -- frozen_string_literal remains opt-in, magic-comment only; YJIT remains opt-in (not on by default); Ractor API instability continues; the bigger overhaul lands in 4.0; RJIT still present but slated for removal (removed in 4.0) |
| ruby | 4.0 | 4.0.0 – 4.0.7 (8) | amd64, arm64 | 4.0.7 | Source build or distro packages; WASI/WebAssembly build continues | ZJIT is explicitly not production-ready; YJIT remains the production JIT choice; The Ractor rewrite is a breaking change: Ractor.yield, Ractor#take, Ractor#close_incoming and Ractor#close_outgoing are all removed in favor of Ractor::Port -- 3.x Ractor code needs porting; RJIT removed entirely (--rjit flag gone); CGI library gutted to just cgi/escape; SortedSet removed from the standard library (now needs a separate gem) |

## nim on linux: available

| Runtime | Major | Versions | Architectures | Downloaded here | OS support at the time | Limitations |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| nim | 0.20 | 0.20.0 – 0.20.2 (2) | amd64, x86 | 0.20.2 | Same as prior 0.x releases; source tarball / distro packages. | Multi-methods are removed from the default dispatch model -- methods are now single-dispatch (first parameter only) unless the program opts back in with --multimethods:on; generic methods are deprecated as 'never worked well'; `typed` as a template/macro result type changes meaning (now 'expression with a type'); code relying on the old meaning must switch to `void`/no result type -- a real source-breaking change on upgrade; Still no ARC/ORC and no destructor-based production memory management; refc remains the only supported default GC; Threads still opt-in and still cannot share a GC heap under refc |
| nim | 1.0 | 1.0.0 – 1.0.10 (6) | amd64, arm64, armv7, x86 | — | Source tarball / distro packages (Arch, Debian/Ubuntu already packaged Nim by this point). | The stability guarantee explicitly excludes a list of modules (concurrency/threadpool, concurrency/atomics, coro, hotcodereloading, ...) -- these can and did keep changing after 1.0; No ARC/ORC yet; refc remains the default and only production GC; threads:on still opt-in; no shared GC heap across threads; No package-level guarantee: third-party Nimble packages are not covered by the 1.0 stability promise, only the compiler and stdlib are |
| nim | 1.2 | 1.2.0 – 1.2.18 (10) | amd64, arm64, armv7, x86 | — | Source tarball / distro packages. | --gc:arc is explicitly experimental in 1.2: the changelog is dominated by ARC crash/segfault/leak fixes (double frees, thread-spawn corruption, exception interaction bugs) -- not recommended for production use yet; --gc:arc has no cycle collector: reference cycles leak by design until ORC (1.4) is available; system.deepcopy and some stdlib code paths do not yet fully support --gc:arc; Still no ORC, no strictFuncs, no top-down type inference; refc remains the default GC for ordinary builds |
| nim | 1.4 | 1.4.0 – 1.4.8 (5) | amd64, arm64, armv7, x86 | — | Source tarball / distro packages. | system.deepcopy must be explicitly re-enabled (--deepcopy:on) under --gc:arc/--gc:orc -- silent behavior change versus refc; Sink inference (part of the move-semantics optimizer) is disabled by default for user code (though still on for the stdlib); has to be turned on explicitly with --sinkInference:on, and mainly matters for --gc:arc/--gc:orc; threadpool's spawn/FlowVar still does not fully work with --gc:arc (open bug fixed only partially in this line); ORC is brand new in this release -- changelog is full of orc-specific crash and memory-corruption fixes (e.g. AsyncHttpClient segfaults under --gc:orc); not yet the recommended default |
| nim | 1.6 | 1.6.0 – 1.6.20 (11) | amd64, arm64, armv7, x86 | 1.6.20 | Source tarball / distro packages; 32-bit RISC-V and armv8l support added as compile targets in this release. | refc is still the compiler's default memory manager for ordinary user code in 1.6 -- ARC/ORC remain opt-in via --gc:arc/--gc:orc, and threads:on is still opt-in (both become the default only in 2.0); Apple Silicon support here is compiler/target support, not necessarily an official prebuilt nim-lang.org macOS/arm64 binary at this point (that is confirmed only on the current install page, not dated precisely); No stdlib HTTP/2 or HTTP/3, no strictDefs, no top-down type inference, no OpenSSL 3 support yet (arrives 2.0); strictFuncs is still experimental and its exact rules change again in 2.0 |
| nim | 2.0 | 2.0.0 – 2.0.16 (9) | amd64, arm64, armv7, x86 | — | Source tarball / distro packages; current install_unix.html additionally lists official arm64 and armv7 prebuilt tarballs whose exact introduction version (somewhere between 1.6 and 2.2) could not be pinned down from the changelogs checked. | ORC-by-default and threads:on-by-default are both breaking changes for code that depended on refc's exact semantics (e.g. shallowCopy/shallow are removed outright for ARC/ORC; use move or copy+assign instead) or assumed threads were off; strictFuncs' definition changed again: it now additionally disallows heap stores through a ref/ptr indirection -- code that passed under 1.x strictFuncs could fail to compile under 2.0's; Several stdlib modules (db_sqlite, db_mysql, db_postgres, db_odbc, smtp, asyncftpclient, md5, sha1, punycode, ...) were removed from the stdlib into separate Nimble packages (db_connector, checksums, etc.) -- code importing them directly now needs an explicit `nimble install`; Destructors must now be `.raises: []` by default (cannot raise) -- existing custom destructors that raised needed rework |
| nim | 2.2 | 2.2.0 – 2.2.12 (7) | amd64, arm64, armv7, x86 | 2.2.12 | Official prebuilt tarballs for x86_64, x86, arm64 (aarch64) and armv7 (armv7l), confirmed present on the current install_unix.html, alongside choosenim and distro packages (Arch, Debian/Ubuntu, Fedora via the Terra repo, FreeBSD, OpenBSD, openSUSE, Void Linux) and an official Docker image. | std/htmlparser is removed from the stdlib into a separate Nimble package -- another stdlib module joins the 2.0 list that already moved out; --experimental:vtables is opt-in and changes method semantics (module-confined dispatch) -- not a drop-in replacement for ordinary methods; Non-var destructors are opt-in only via -d:nimPreviewNonVarDestructor in 2.2 -- existing var-based =destroy hooks are not yet forced to migrate; Still no HTTP/2 or HTTP/3 client in the stdlib as of 2.2.12 (the latest patch on this line checked) |

## zig on freebsd: available

| Runtime | Major | Versions | Architectures | Downloaded here | OS support at the time | Limitations |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| zig | 0.4 | 0.4.0 | amd64 | — | added this release | self-hosted compiler and package manager both still future roadmap items, unusable; no native macOS/Mach-O linker (must shell out) -- roadmap item; only Tier 1 targets "thoroughly tested"; other libcs planned but not present |
| zig | 0.5 | 0.5.0 | amd64 | — | continuing Tier support | self-hosted compiler still not the default (C++ "stage1" only); no package manager; iOS and Android support brand new and lower-tier |
| zig | 0.6 | 0.6.0 | amd64 | — |  | Windows 7 no longer supported (8.1+ required); self-hosted compiler still not the default; no package manager; zig cc still recommended only as a low-level escape hatch; --c-source / build-system API preferred |
| zig | 0.7 | 0.7.0 – 0.7.1 (2) | amd64 | — |  | self-hosted compiler (stage2) still cannot be used for normal builds (stage1 remains default until 0.10.0); no package manager (tracked as roadmap "Package Manager Status"); the 0.7.0 release notes explicitly say "These Release Notes are Incomplete" |
| zig | 0.8 | 0.8.0 – 0.8.1 (2) | amd64 | — |  | stage2 (self-hosted) still not the default compiler for .zig source (stays stage1 until 0.10.0); arm64 macOS builds have no CI coverage yet, considered experimental; no package manager yet |
| zig | 0.9 | 0.9.0 – 0.9.1 (2) | amd64 | — |  | ARM64 Windows binaries unverified by the Zig team; aarch64-macos has no CI, reliability unknown, flaky OOM failures reported for related CI targets; still stage1-compiled by default (stage2 not default until 0.10.0); no package manager |
| zig | 0.10 | 0.10.0 | amd64 | — |  | async/await "is not done yet" in the self-hosted compiler -- users of async/await cannot upgrade to stage2 until it's complete (it never was; async regressed permanently in 0.11.0); stage2 has fresh bugs, missing or less-helpful compile errors, and "falls short of stage1" on C ABI compatibility, C translation, several codegen backends, and the self-hosted linker (MachO/COFF/ELF); macOS 10 (Catalina) no longer supported; still no package manager |
| zig | 0.11 | 0.11.0 | amd64 | — |  | async functions regressed with this release and were never fixed in the self-hosted compiler (LLVM cannot optimize them well; async function pointers prevent the stack size from being known); TLS client has no server counterpart and "has not yet been fuzz-tested" / "use it at your own risk"; package manager has no official registry and no optional dependencies or first-class binary-dependency support yet; Windows 8.1 no longer supported (10+ required) |
| zig | 0.12 | 0.12.0 – 0.12.1 (2) | amd64 | — |  | Async/Await formally flagged as an unresolved roadmap item: "their future in the Zig language is unclear" due to LLVM optimization limits and async-function-pointer stack-size problems; "Bring-Your-Own-OS API Layer Regressed"; package manager still pre-1.0, usable mainly for "simple pure-Zig projects" |
| zig | 0.13 | 0.13.0 | amd64 | — |  | small release focused on stdlib renames and a security fix rather than new capability; async/await status unchanged from 0.12 (still broken in the self-hosted compiler, future undecided) |
| zig | 0.15 | 0.15.1 – 0.15.2 (2) | amd64, arm64, armv7, ppc64, ppc64le, riscv64 | — | 14.0; dynamically-linked FreeBSD libc now supported when cross-compiling | async/await no longer exists as language syntax; existing code must be rewritten by hand, no automatic migration; "Writergate" is a sweeping breaking change to nearly all I/O code (custom format methods now require "{f}"; format strings/options removed from format methods); incremental compilation still experimental with "known bugs" that "can lead to miscompilations or incorrect compile errors"; Linux minimum jumped from 3.16 to 5.10, dropping support for older kernels |
| zig | 0.16 | 0.16.0 | amd64, arm64, armv7, ppc64le, riscv64 | — | 14.0 | no HTTP/2 or HTTP/3 client (std.http remains HTTP/1.1-only); std.crypto.tls remains a TLS 1.3-only client with no server implementation; Io.Evented backend does not yet implement networking, and Io.net has no non-IP networking, so evented (single-threaded async) I/O and real networking cannot yet be combined; aarch64-macos and aarch64-windows remain Tier 2, still not Tier 1, roughly five years after Apple Silicon macOS support first appeared (0.8.0); std.Thread.Pool removed outright; code depending on it must migrate to std.Io.Group/async |

2 recorded gaps, e.g.: 0.14 amd64: ziglang.org's index has no freebsd/amd64 (x86_64-freebsd) build for either 0.14.0 or 0.14.1, the only two patches in this major. x86_64-freebsd is present continuously from 0.11.0 through 0.13.0 and comes back at 0.15.1, so this looks like a real gap in the 0.14 line.; 0.16 ppc64: ziglang.org's index has no freebsd/ppc64 (powerpc64-freebsd) build for 0.16.0, the only patch in this major so far. powerpc64-freebsd was newly added at 0.15.1 and is present in both 0.15.1 and 0.15.2, so its absence in 0.16.0 looks like a dropped target rather than one not yet built -- worth re-checking once a 0.16.x patch release exists.

| zig | 0.7 | 0.7.0 – 0.7.1 (2) | amd64, arm64, armv7, riscv64, x86 | 0.7.1 | 3.16+; glibc 2.32, musl 1.2.1, mingw-w64 8.0.0 | self-hosted compiler (stage2) still cannot be used for normal builds (stage1 remains default until 0.10.0); no package manager (tracked as roadmap "Package Manager Status"); the 0.7.0 release notes explicitly say "These Release Notes are Incomplete" |
| zig | 0.8 | 0.8.0 – 0.8.1 (2) | amd64, arm64, armv7, riscv64, x86 | 0.8.1 | 3.16+; glibc 2.33 | stage2 (self-hosted) still not the default compiler for .zig source (stays stage1 until 0.10.0); arm64 macOS builds have no CI coverage yet, considered experimental; no package manager yet |
| zig | 0.9 | 0.9.0 – 0.9.1 (2) | amd64, arm64, armv7, riscv64, x86 | 0.9.1 | 3.16+; glibc 2.34; io_uring wrappers added | ARM64 Windows binaries unverified by the Zig team; aarch64-macos has no CI, reliability unknown, flaky OOM failures reported for related CI targets; still stage1-compiled by default (stage2 not default until 0.10.0); no package manager |
| zig | 0.10 | 0.10.0 – 0.10.1 (2) | amd64, arm64, armv7, riscv64, x86 | 0.10.0, 0.10.1 | 3.16+; glibc 2.34; default C ABI on Linux changed from gnu to none | async/await "is not done yet" in the self-hosted compiler -- users of async/await cannot upgrade to stage2 until it's complete (it never was; async regressed permanently in 0.11.0); stage2 has fresh bugs, missing or less-helpful compile errors, and "falls short of stage1" on C ABI compatibility, C translation, several codegen backends, and the self-hosted linker (MachO/COFF/ELF); macOS 10 (Catalina) no longer supported; still no package manager |
| zig | 0.11 | 0.11.0 | amd64, arm64, armv7, ppc64le, riscv64, x86 | 0.11.0 | 3.16+; glibc 2.34, musl 1.2.4 | async functions regressed with this release and were never fixed in the self-hosted compiler (LLVM cannot optimize them well; async function pointers prevent the stack size from being known); TLS client has no server counterpart and "has not yet been fuzz-tested" / "use it at your own risk"; package manager has no official registry and no optional dependencies or first-class binary-dependency support yet; Windows 8.1 no longer supported (10+ required) |
| zig | 0.12 | 0.12.0 – 0.12.1 (2) | amd64, arm64, armv7, ppc64le, riscv64, x86 | 0.12.1 | 3.16+; glibc 2.38, musl 1.2.4, mingw-w64 updated; LLVM 17 | Async/Await formally flagged as an unresolved roadmap item: "their future in the Zig language is unclear" due to LLVM optimization limits and async-function-pointer stack-size problems; "Bring-Your-Own-OS API Layer Regressed"; package manager still pre-1.0, usable mainly for "simple pure-Zig projects" |
| zig | 0.13 | 0.13.0 | amd64, arm64, armv7, ppc64le, riscv64, x86 | 0.13.0 | 3.16+; glibc 2.39, musl 1.2.5; LLVM 18 | small release focused on stdlib renames and a security fix rather than new capability; async/await status unchanged from 0.12 (still broken in the self-hosted compiler, future undecided) |
| zig | 0.14 | 0.14.0 – 0.14.1 (2) | amd64, arm64, armv7, loong64, ppc64le, riscv64, s390x, x86 | 0.14.1 | kernel headers refreshed to 6.13.4; glibc 2.41, musl 1.2.5 | incremental compilation not ready for default use ("None of them are generally ready for use yet"); aarch64-macos and aarch64-windows still Tier 2, without CI parity with x86_64-linux's Tier 1; std.builtin.Type field renames break existing reflection/comptime code; @fence removed outright in favor of explicit atomic ordering barriers |
| zig | 0.15 | 0.15.1 – 0.15.2 (2) | amd64, arm64, armv7, loong64, ppc64le, riscv64, s390x, x86 | 0.15.2 | 5.10 (raised from the long-standing 3.16+ floor) | async/await no longer exists as language syntax; existing code must be rewritten by hand, no automatic migration; "Writergate" is a sweeping breaking change to nearly all I/O code (custom format methods now require "{f}"; format strings/options removed from format methods); incremental compilation still experimental with "known bugs" that "can lead to miscompilations or incorrect compile errors"; Linux minimum jumped from 3.16 to 5.10, dropping support for older kernels |
| zig | 0.16 | 0.16.0 | amd64, arm64, armv7, loong64, ppc64le, riscv64, s390x, x86 | 0.16.0 | 5.10; headers refreshed to 6.19; glibc 2.43, musl 1.2.5 | no HTTP/2 or HTTP/3 client (std.http remains HTTP/1.1-only); std.crypto.tls remains a TLS 1.3-only client with no server implementation; Io.Evented backend does not yet implement networking, and Io.net has no non-IP networking, so evented (single-threaded async) I/O and real networking cannot yet be combined; aarch64-macos and aarch64-windows remain Tier 2, still not Tier 1, roughly five years after Apple Silicon macOS support first appeared (0.8.0); std.Thread.Pool removed outright; code depending on it must migrate to std.Io.Group/async |
